### ✅ The season record region — thumbnails, one control, and a panel that was finally measured `[P2 · S · Opus5-High]`
*Items B, C and D of the completion plan's Task 1.2, built 2026-08-27.*

**B — banners are real thumbnails.** Three of the record's six cells are pictures and read *"image cached and serving"*, which scans as three more deadlines with wordy values and asserts the one thing a reader would rather see. The image replaces the sentence in the same grid cell, so the six stay peers. A stored URL that will not load is now its own warned state rather than being indistinguishable from a served one.

**C — the close target went from 52px to the header's full width.** Collapsed, the strip is click-anywhere at 147px tall; open, the only way back was a 52px Done button in the same y-position. Harkirat chose the header's empty space. ⚠️ Deliberately NOT `role="button"` on the header — it contains the Live/Next switch and Done, and interactive nesting is invalid ARIA; Done remains the keyboard path. The handler tests its event target rather than relying on `stopPropagation` per child, so a control added later cannot silently start closing the panel.

**D — measured, and it is fine.** 1,112 × 602, field rows on a consistent label and input axis; the only flagged spread is different row TYPES. compact-I's own confession was *"I called it 'fine' three times and never measured it once"* — this is the same verdict with a reading behind it.

**Analytics** — three of the four tiles repeated the masthead's three figures verbatim. Verified zero overlap.

**Review** — the masthead counted every blocker and the footer named the first one only. All are listed now. ⚠️ **Its composition was otherwise left alone deliberately** — it is ahead of the mockup (typed confirmation, real export gate, per-op transactions), and the mockup's own sample data renders `[object Object]` in four places.

✅ **Verified by the item's own test, measured rather than eyeballed.** The plan's condition is *"blur the text on any two realms; if they are the same image, it is not done"* — implemented as a geometry fingerprint: every bordered block over 200×40px in the first 1,400px of each realm, quantised to a 40px grid, text ignored entirely. **Six distinct signatures across six realms, zero identical pairs.** ⚠️ And the regression the Armory change could have caused was checked rather than assumed: the editor has its OWN nested bed grid, so with a build open the outer grid is one column at 1,158px and the inner one is `752px 340px` with the preview column still 340px wide and visible.

It is realm-level now — **MP 125 · 68 · 60 · 106 and DMZ 8 · 7 · 6 · 0, which add to the old totals exactly** — and BulkView's own switch is gone, because two controls over one quantity can disagree. - **🖱️ LIVE CLICK-TEST OWED on the `index.js` restructure — every interactive surface, not a spot-check** `[P1 · M · Sonnet5-High · 👤Harkirat runs the test]` *(the model tag is for triaging and fixing whatever the test surfaces — premise is low once a click reports a concrete symptom, but the deliberation load is high because a fault could sit in any of ~93 branches; the test itself is Harkirat's, not a model's.)* *(filed 2026-08-13 17:30 EDT, the same turn the restructure was committed.)* v3.16.0-pre moved the **entire** `interactionCreate` router plus the whole boot sequence into new modules, so the blast radius is every button, dropdown and modal in the bot — not just the colours slice that started it. **Only Harkirat can close this: a click-test cannot be run from the CLI.** The dev bot (`node --watch --env-file=.env.dev index.js`) is the surface; nothing is deployed, the VM still runs `main`.
  - **What the automated checks already prove, so don't re-do it by hand:** 93 `custom_id` branches before the move and 93 after (none lost or gained) · all 171 relative `require` paths resolve · every module loads under Node · boot reaches both `handleBotReady()` lines · **the whole branch diff was read end to end** by partitioning it — 3,934 of 4,339 deleted lines are exact moves once the `require('./x')`→`('../x')` rewrite is normalised, leaving 73 deleted-only non-comment lines, all explicable, nothing lost · **prefix conformance and the admin gate** are permanent tests (`scripts/handlerRouting.test.js` checks 9 and 10) · **the two cooldowns and their concurrency behaviour** are permanent tests (`scripts/handlerState.test.js`, see the ⏱️ bullet below). **What none of it can prove is that a branch BEHAVES the same when it actually runs against Discord** — a moved block referencing something that is no longer in scope, a reply that renders wrong, an interaction token used after it expired. That is what the click-test is for.
  - **Highest-risk surfaces, in order:** `/manage` — every page, add/bulk/edit/delete, purge, wipe, **undo**, and the admin/announce flows, since its short-lived token Maps moved and a broken one looks like a confirm button that silently does nothing · the five View Colors buttons, incl. **Refresh twice inside 10s** (the second must be cooldown-blocked, which is the direct proof its Map survived intact) · `/settings` toggles · loadout pagination · nav bar · draw-prices region + subpage.
  - ⏱️ **STATEFUL / TEMPORAL — TWO of the three mechanisms are now under automated test; the third is the one genuinely owed (updated 2026-08-14 10:20 EDT).** ⚠️ This bullet used to read *"the part NO automated check here can reach … none has been exercised even once"*, and that is no longer true. `scripts/handlerState.test.js` (7 checks, wired into `npm test`, each proven falsifiable by mutation) drives the real handlers and covers **(1)** the 10s Refresh Colors cooldown (`colorsRefreshCooldowns`, module-private to `handlers/colors.js`) — rejection, the "Slow down" reply, per-user keying, and two simultaneous clicks resolving to exactly one — and **(2)** the 600ms generic anti-spam guard (`interactionCooldowns`, `handlers/router.js`) — swallow-by-`deferUpdate()`, per-user keying, window EXPIRY (it throttles, it does not latch), and the same concurrency property. It needs no Discord and no Mongo because `attachGuildPolicy` returns early when `guildId` is null, `resolvePanelActor` returns early when the panel's target is the clicker, and a mock whose `deferUpdate` throws a sentinel halts execution immediately after the store is written.
  - 🔴 **STILL OWED — `/manage`'s FIVE short-lived Maps, and only a human click can close it.** `manageUndoStore`, `pendingSeasonWipes`, `pendingManageDeletes`, `pendingManageEdits`, `pendingBulkDeletes` — all module-private to `handlers/manage/shared.js` (moved there from the old single-file handlers/manage.js by the stage-2 directory split, 2026-08-14 17:33 EDT), all verified single-instance statically (which proves ONE INSTANCE EXISTS and nothing about whether it works). They resist the mock approach the two cooldowns yielded to for one specific reason: **a manage confirm/undo flow only reaches its Map after a real Mongo mutation**, so there is no early-return to stop at and no sentinel that fires before the DB. Stubbing that out would be re-implementing `/manage`, not testing it. **A broken one looks exactly like a confirm button that silently does nothing** — no error, no log, the panel just sits there. **Run it on the dev bot** (`node --watch --env-file=.env.dev index.js`, local Mongo, never prod). Each step is two clicks with something in between, because a single click can pass on a Map that is written and never read:
    1. **Undo** — `/manage` → Loadouts → delete one entry → press **Undo** → the entry comes back. Then delete another and press Undo **after ~15 minutes**; it must decline cleanly rather than restore stale data or throw. (Read-back after write is the actual assertion; the delete alone proves nothing.)
    2. **Delete confirm** (`pendingManageDeletes`) — start a delete, then **navigate to another page and back** before confirming. The pending token must survive the re-render, or expire in a way that says so.
    3. **Bulk delete** (`pendingBulkDeletes`) — same shape, and additionally start a bulk delete, then start a SECOND one before confirming the first: the second must not silently inherit the first's target set.
    4. **Season wipe** (`pendingSeasonWipes`) — the two-step confirm, then **Cancel**, then immediately start it again. A stale key here means the second attempt confirms without a second confirmation.
    5. **Edit** (`pendingManageEdits`) — open an edit modal, dismiss it without submitting, reopen it. The reopened modal must carry the current values, not the dismissed draft. ⚠️ **Two users are not needed, but two SESSIONS are:** every one of these Maps is keyed per user, so the interesting failure is the same user with two panels open at once. Where practical, open `/manage` twice and interleave.
  - **Also confirm the boot path itself:** all commands still register (a wrong `commands/` path in `bot/registry.js` registers an EMPTY set with no error), and a `./scripts/deploy.sh`-style restart still reports as a **deploy** rather than "unattended" (the `.restart-reason` marker moved too). Both were re-anchored against `..` and both fail silently.

  - 🆕 **COMPOUNDED AGAIN 2026-08-14 17:21 EDT by stage 2 (mechanical split done, not yet merged) — a THIRD reason this test is owed, and this one is a genuine behaviour change, not just a file move.** the old single-file handlers/manage.js (2,416 lines) became a directory (`handlers/manage/index.js` + `shared.js` + one module per page: `draws.js`, `calendar.js`, `loadouts.js`, `patchnotes.js`, `season.js`, `admins.js`, `announcements.js`), and all ~25 confirm/cancel/undo prompts that used to call discord.js's raw `interaction.update()` now go through a new `prompt()` helper (`handlers/manage/shared.js`) that routes through `sendV2Payload` instead — a real Components-V2 render, not a legacy content+buttons message. **What is already proven, so don't redo it by hand:** `npm test` is green (routing-contract checks adapted for the directory shape, the state/concurrency checks, `manageActions.test.js`'s 57-action harness, all 26 hook self-tests); the dev bot boots clean under the new layout (Mongo connects, gateway authenticates, no errors); every string literal in the deleted file was traced into its new home via a git-diffed conservation check (one paraphrased comment detail was restored, nothing else lost). **What a human click still has to prove, ON TOP of everything the two bullets above already ask for:** that a `prompt()`-rendered confirm/cancel/undo message actually LOOKS right (Text Display + Action Row under the V2 flag, not broken formatting) and that Discord accepts the payload shape at all — this is the one thing no static check or dry boot can verify, since it depends on Discord's own API validation of the exact JSON `prompt()` sends. **Deferred 2026-08-14 17:15 EDT** (Harkirat: "i'm not available to click-test at the moment so that part needs to be deferred") — `handlers/router.js`'s cancel-never-reschedule safety net is being KEPT (not deleted) specifically until this happens; see that file's own comment on the block.

- **🚦 Pre-merge gates fire ONE AT A TIME — a single `gh pr merge` can burn a round-trip per violation** `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` *(filed 2026-08-09 12:25 EDT, Harkirat: "this is just inefficient and unoptimized when both these could have been caught at the same time".)* **Model pick reasoning:** premise **Low** (mechanism observed live, evidence below) · deliberation **Medium** (the fix touches tracked enforcement hooks, and every hook needs a passing self-test — `run-all-tests.sh` fails if one is missing). Grid → `Sonnet5-Medium`.
  - **Observed live, 2026-08-09 12:20–12:22 EDT, merging PR #105.** Attempt 1 (`gh pr merge 105 --squash --delete-branch`) violated **two** gates at once — no DEVLOG entry **and** no `--body`. Only `release-ready-check` reported. Attempt 2 was the same command with the DEVLOG written and the **identical** missing `--body`; only then did `squash-trailer-gate` fire. Attempt 3 succeeded. **Three round-trips for one merge, where one report listing both would have taken one.**
  - **Root cause:** the two gates are registered as **separate `PreToolUse` matcher groups** (both `"matcher": "Bash"`) in `.claude/settings.json`, and the first `deny` short-circuits the rest. Neither hook is wrong; the *composition* is. Every additional merge-time gate makes this linearly worse.
  - **Shape of the fix (do not just merge the scripts):** keep each gate its own testable script — that is what makes them individually verifiable — but have the merge-time path **run all applicable gates and aggregate every failure into one denial message**. Either a thin `pre-merge-gate.sh` dispatcher that invokes each check and concatenates non-empty reasons, or have the first-firing gate run its siblings when the command matches `gh pr merge`.
  - **How to verify:** construct a `gh pr merge` command violating BOTH conditions (missing DEVLOG entry + no `--body`) and confirm a **single** denial names **both**. ⚠️ Add/extend the self-tests — `.claude/hooks/*.test.sh` coverage is enforced, and a new dispatcher needs its own. ⚠️ Test the way hooks actually RUN (non-interactive shell); this machine's interactive aliases have hidden two hook bugs before.

- **🔎 The sweep's angle detection was WRONG on its first live fire** `[P1 · S]` ⚠️ **Its `Stop` half is RETIRED as of 2026-09-06 22:26 EDT** — the hook is `commit-completeness-sweep.sh` and fires on commit/merge now, so the *does it fire at `Stop`* half of this entry is history. **The angle-detection defect below is UNCHANGED and still open**: it is about what the harness hands the hook, not about which event invokes it. *(re-scoped from the original "does it fire at all" reminder, which is now answered.)*

  **Answered half:** it fired, unprompted, on a real completion claim. The wiring works.

  **Open half, and the reason this stays P1:** it reported **all five angles as un-taken** on a session that had demonstrably run every one — measured afterwards, the detectors match at transcript lines 88, 195, 1409, 1410 and 2073 of 3902. Re-running the hook by hand against that same file suppressed all five correctly, so the matching logic is fine and **the fault is in what the harness handed it**. Root cause UNKNOWN; do not guess at it, reproduce it.
  **Reproduce like this:** make a completion claim on a branch with changes, capture what the hook reports, then run `bash .claude/hooks/commit-completeness-sweep.sh main <transcript> commit` by hand against the path it names and compare. The hook now prints the transcript path and line count when it sees zero Bash entries, which is the diagnostic that was missing the first time.

  ⚠️ **Mitigated, not fixed.** A transcript with zero Bash tool_use entries now reports "ANGLE DETECTION COULD NOT RUN" instead of listing every angle — because *"I cannot see the session"* and *"no angle was taken"* are different answers, and reporting all five is the fires-on-everything behaviour that gets a gate dismissed unread. That makes the failure visible; it does not explain it.

  **Two real bugs found while investigating, both fixed:** `claim-detect.sh` was resolved from `${BASH_SOURCE[0]}` *after* the script `cd`s to `$REPO`, re-anchoring a relative invocation path to the wrong tree (breaks under a worktree); and the Bash-scoping is weaker than documented — JSONL puts a whole assistant message on ONE line, so prose sharing a message with any Bash call still leaks through.

- `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-Low]` **Live-test the View Colors "Refresh Colors" button — the `sendV2Payload` attachments-replacement fix.** *(Its own entry as of 2026-08-10 22:07 EDT. It was briefly folded into the `/manage` live-test item below and that was WRONG — see the correction note.)*
  - 🛑 **CORRECTION, Harkirat caught it 2026-08-10 22:07 EDT: this has NOTHING to do with `/manage`.** It was folded into the `/manage`/`/settings` live-test entry at 21:55 EDT on the reasoning that all three were "pending human clicks" — but the entry's TITLE then named `/manage` and `/settings` and not View Colors, so it read as though the attachments fix were a `/manage` concern. **A shared *chore type* is not a shared *subject*, and bundling on the former while titling on the latter manufactures a relationship that does not exist.** Verified before un-bundling: **every** call site that passes `files` to `sendV2Payload` is a colours path — `handlers/colors.js` (5 call sites, one per view/variant/page/refresh handler — moved out of `commands/colors.js` in the 2026-08-13 index.js split) (the `colors_view|`, `colors_variant_`, `colors_page_`, `colors_refresh_` handlers) — and **`/manage` never passes `files` at all.** `utils/sendV2Payload.js` states the same in its own comment: *"Callers that DON'T pass `files` are unaffected (attachments field omitted entirely, same as before)."*
  - **What the fix is.** `utils/sendV2Payload.js:27-30` sends `attachments: []` whenever `files` is present. Discord's edit-message API otherwise has no instruction about a message's EXISTING attachments when new ones upload in the same edit, so it can retain the old ones instead of replacing them. The original live symptom, quoted in the source comment: *"Refresh Colors said it found new colors, but the panel kept showing the OLD swatch images until switching pages and back."*
  - 📍 **Where to click.** The button lives on the **View Colors panel**, not on `/colors` specifically: built at `utils/colorPaletteView.js:265-269` as `colors_refresh_<source>_<subpage>|<userId>|<g|s>`, handled at `index.js:2038`. **The panel has two entry points** — the `/colors` command (`handlers/colors.js`) and `/settings` → the **"View Colors"** button (`commands/settings.js:165`, custom_id `colors_view|`, handled at `index.js:1834`). Both build the same panel through the same `sendV2Payload` call, so either door exercises the fix. **Pass condition:** new swatches appear immediately on Refresh, with no page-switch needed.
  - ⚠️ **The test surface GREW after the note was written.** The custom_id's trailing `|g` / `|s` is the **global-vs-server variant**, which did not exist in 2026-07-13/14 when this was filed — it arrived with **v3.2.0's per-server profile colours**. So "click Refresh once" is no longer the whole test: **both variants need a click**, and the server variant is the newer and less-exercised of the two.
  - ⚠️ **Its original trigger — "re-test the next time this area is touched" — has since fired repeatedly with nobody watching it.** v3.2.0's per-server profile colours and v3.3.0's nameplate/deco WebP pivot both shipped straight through that render path. **A conditional trigger with no owner is not a trigger**, which is why it now has its own tagged entry with a stated pass condition instead of a "re-test sometime" note on a roadmap.

🔴 **PIN 60, added by chat %s — the tier board's weapon cards and builds read as dead, and it is THREE faults, not one.** Harkirat: *"clicking on these weapons or their builds in this layout does absolutely nothing."* Traced in `portal/ui/armory.js` rather than taken at face value:
1. **The weapon card header genuinely has no handler.** `WeaponGroup` renders `.bgrp` / `.bgrp-h` as a plain `<div>` — no `onClick`, no `role`, no `tabindex` (`armory.js:127`). Clicking `BAL-27` does nothing, exactly as reported.
2. **The build chip DOES have one, and its entire effect is off-screen.** `BuildChip` binds `onClick=${() => onPick(b.weaponName)}` (`:105`) and the Rack's `onPick` is `setWeaponFilter` (`:1317`) — so clicking a build filters the **Manifest**, which sits thousands of pixels below the tier board. A click that worked is indistinguishable from one that did not. This is `feedback_affordance_distance_not_absence` inverted: not a control below the fold, but a CONSEQUENCE below the fold.
3. **`role="button"` + `tabindex="0"` with no `onKeyDown`** (`:103`) — keyboard-reachable and keyboard-inert, against the rule `manifest.js` states in its own comment (*"a role=button must answer both"*). **Mine, mechanical, no fork.**

**Fault 2 is the fork and it is his:** should clicking a build open its row editor the way a Manifest row does, or filter-and-scroll, or both? **Do not guess** — the answer decides whether fault 1 is "wire the header to the same thing" or "the header is a label and only builds are actionable". Pairs with `pmtvq5x6k` in group G, which asks what a card is even telling you.


**HIS, and shown before asked:** the DATE/TBD treatment · what a weapon card carries and how tall it is · what the command bar becomes · whether "one date"/"a window" goes or gets explained · whether the Access header survives once he has seen the current build.

**Verify:** each group closes on Harkirat's eye against the CURRENT build at 1268×779 on the signed-in dev portal, not on a computed-value check — that is the failure mode round 1 was called out for.

  **Two harness facts worth more than they look, both measured tonight:** hash-only routing (`location.hash = '#/x'`) leaves the previous realm's masthead mounted with no realm body — use a full `location.href` reload with a ≥1,800ms settle; and synthetic `mouseenter`/`focus` do not trigger `tips.js`, so a tooltip needs a real hover, at which point the pane's coordinate frame is 800×562 against an emulated 1282×900 and coordinates need scaling by 0.624.



### ✅ `[CLOSED 2026-09-09 15:42 EDT]` The v3 launch scope is enumerated now — `docs/ROADMAP.md` § 🚀 v3.0.0 — THE LAUNCH CHECKLIST

*Filed 2026-09-09 15:36 EDT, after a handoff described finishing the portal plan's step 4 as though it finished v3.* Harkirat: *"id hardly call finishing step 4 the end of v3 pre release… and i've likely missed listing many things here."*

🔴 **AND TWO OF THIS ENTRY'S OWN CLAIMS WERE WRONG, BOTH ASSERTED FROM A SEARCH RATHER THAN A READ — corrected 2026-09-09 15:42 EDT.** It said *"`awk` over ROADMAP.md returns nothing, there is no v3 section"*: **there is one**, at `### v3 (next MAJOR version)`, and my pattern looked for `^## `. It also said the roadmap *"still lists splitting `index.js` as part of v3"*: **it is struck through and marked SHIPPED**, and my grep had hit body lines under the struck header. **An absence reported from a pattern that never matched is not an absence** — which is the same class as everything else this entry describes. The real gap was narrower and still real: the scope was spread across four files with no single checklist. **There is one now.** The launch scope is scattered across `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/db-deferred-list.md`, root `CLAUDE.md` and `.impeccable/`, and the only way to assemble it is a keyword sweep for `SHIPS WITH v3|v3 LAUNCH|pre-launch|before launch` — which finds items by how they happen to be WORDED, so anything phrased differently is invisible to it.

🔴 **The exposure is STRUCTURAL and the measured instance count is ZERO — both halves matter.** Replaying this session’s 60 batched commands: no `git push`, `git commit`, `git tag` or `gh pr merge` was ever routed through a batch, and `rg-flag-guard` fires on 0 of the 60 when fed them directly. So nothing has evaded a gate yet; what is true is that nothing would stop it, and the two hooks that WERE bitten only revealed it because their job is to fire often. ⚠️ **Do not read the zero as safety** — the nudges’ own count was zero too, right up until a full day’s prose searching went un-interrupted.

### `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` The product map is DECLARATIVE ONLY — no node verifies itself against reality

The verify half — the `signal` / `regex` / `section_contains` / file checks the docs describe, which produce a `convergence` / `divergence` verdict that **overrides the hand-declared status** — is not written anywhere yet.

A `section_contains` check on `scripts/buildLegalPages.js`'s `SPECS` array would turn that from a filed item into a reconciler verdict with file:line evidence — which is the entire point of the map.

**Do:** add a verify block to the nodes that have a checkable claim, starting with `commands-page`. **Verify:** `map status` reports a non-zero *Verified by reality*, and `map explain commands-page` prints a divergence with evidence.

### `[P3 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` A separate "Pings" output style for supervised long runs — Harkirat's idea, not mine

**Filed 2026-09-04 10:00 EDT.** Raised by Harkirat 2026-09-04: *"honestly the pings style seems like it's own output style, rather than trying to manipulate 'silent mode' to work for all scenarios."*

Silent mode resolves the conflict between *zero narration* and his 2026-08-19 request — *"keep me updated with 1 line updates on major status changes"* — by allowing an unprompted line only when blocked or when the approved approach turned out wrong. That is a compromise. A second style would let him pick per-session instead: silent when he is away, pings when he is supervising.

**Do:** only if the compromise proves wrong in practice. Author `~/.claude/output-styles/pings.md` sharing Silent's four checks and shape rules, differing only in the interstitial-line policy (one line per completed unit, no elaboration).

**Verify:** the two files share their check and shape sections verbatim, so a fix to one is a fix to both — or the shared part moves to `.claude/rules/` and both reference it.

**Do not build it pre-emptively.** It is a real idea, not an agreed one.

- **Nothing detects a `status: live` plan that has become a record of finished work** `[P3 · S · Sonnet5-High]` — filed 2026-09-03 09:18 EDT. `docs-audit` enforces that a `superseded` file names a `superseded_by:` target; it cannot see that a LIVE file has gone retrospective. `REVIEW-PROMPT.md` is the third to do this, and it sat `live` with an executable command block whose commands now exit 2 — a superseded document's code block is still a code block. **Do:** consider a check — a plan whose §L row is closed, or whose quoted commands fail, is a candidate. ⚠️ Speculative; the cost of a false positive on a legitimately-live plan may exceed the benefit. **Verify by:** the check firing on `REVIEW-PROMPT.md` as it stood this morning and staying silent on the conformance plan.

### `[closed]` Original entry — the INJECTED tier has no budget, and the workflow this repo mandates bypasses the rules layer
*Filed 2026-09-02 10:59 EDT. Measured this session with `.claude/hooks/instructions-loaded-audit.sh`, built the same day. Full audit (measurements, refutations, falsifiers) is at `~/.claude/plans/worktree-session-ready-await-vivid-naur.md` — **outside the repo**, so this entry is the reachable copy.*

**Measured.** Always-injected before any work: root `CLAUDE.md` **90,040 B** · global `CLAUDE.md`+`RTK.md` 37,718 · `docs/SESSION-START.md` 30,901 · the loaded slice of `MEMORY.md` 25,000 · `self-check.sh` 3,798 **per prompt** ≈ **185 KB / ~46k tokens**. `.claude/rules/` totals **651,223 B** (`accent-and-colors.md` alone 144,650) and injects a median **72,420 B**, worst **187,237 B**, when a read triggers it. Only `MEMORY.md` has a budget or a gate — and it is the smallest of them.

**Three things the instrument settled, none of which was knowable by reading.** ① Path-scoped rules fire on `Read`/`Edit` only: `rg`, a `python3` heredoc read, and a `python3` heredoc write all produced **zero** loads against a positive control that fired. The batching contract's own mandated tools bypass the layer. ② `Read` with `limit: 12` injected the **entire** 9,256 B rule — cost is not proportional to how much you read. ③ A rule loads **once per session**, not per read (`bot/lifecycle.js` matches two rules; only the un-loaded one logged), so 651 KB is a cumulative ceiling rather than a per-access bill.

**Do.** (a) `memory-index-check.sh` gates **bytes only** against a locally-invented 40,000 while the platform's law is *"first 200 lines OR first 25KB, whichever comes first"* — make it gate both, at 25,000. Today bytes bind at line 127 (185 lines), so a shrink that shortens lines could cross the LINE limit silently with the gate still green. (b) Trim the 124 index entries to the ~150-char target `anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory` itself specifies (median is 199, max 686) = **−8,499 B**, and move `SILENT MODE` (~5,400 B) to the instruction tier where an instruction belongs — **13,899 B recovered against a 10,147 B overage, nothing deleted** — then delete the `@`-import per its own stated removal condition. (c) Give `.claude/rules/` a per-file byte budget and a gate, and split the four oversized rules into a small injected TRAP plus a reference doc under `docs/reference/`, which `ctx-index-refresh.sh` already indexes.

**Verify:** the visible `**MEMORY-INDEX-END**` line is in a fresh session's context **with the import removed** (⚠️ it was an HTML comment until 2026-09-03 20:13 EDT and therefore could never appear — this verify condition was unsatisfiable as written); a 201-line index under 25 KB **fails** `npm test`; `bash .claude/hooks/instructions-loaded-audit.sh --report` shows no single rule over the new per-file budget; and the `docs-audit` conservation rule confirms every moved paragraph appears at its destination.

### `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-High]` Six hooks are blind to the `python3` heredoc the batching contract mandates
*Filed 2026-09-02 10:59 EDT.*

Registered on `Edit|Write` only, so a heredoc write never reaches them: `clock-inject` · `defer-in-place-guard` · `batch-edit-guard` · `spec-handoff-coauthor-nudge` · `typos-check`, plus one inline hook. Sharpest case: `timestamp-check` is `PreToolUse: Edit|Write` but `PostToolUse: Edit|Write|Bash`, so **the placeholder autofix cannot fire on a heredoc at all** — only the after-the-fact advisory does. That is the hole the four placeholder stamps came through on 2026-09-01, and it is still open.

🔴 **The stated reason for not covering Bash is false, and this was proven rather than argued.** Root `CLAUDE.md` says the autofix is *"deliberately not registered `PreToolUse` on `Bash` (rewriting a shell command mangles it)"*. Piping a synthetic payload into `rtk hook claude` returns `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecisionReason":"RTK auto-rewrite","updatedInput":{"command":"rtk git status"}}}` — **every Bash call on this machine already has its command rewritten via `updatedInput`, and has for months.** Correct that sentence in `CLAUDE.md` in the same change.

✅ **DONE 2026-09-02 11:44 EDT for the timestamp hook** — registered `Edit|Write|Bash`; it already read `.tool_input.command` and already rewrote every string in `.tool_input`, so the matcher was the only thing in the way. Verified live: a `python3` heredoc carrying `11:xx` landed on disk reading `11:42 EDT`. 57/57 in its own suite; `run-all-tests.sh` reports 35 passed, 0 failed. Two `PreToolUse` hooks now return `updatedInput` for the same Bash call (rtk's rewrite and this substitution) and the documented precedence covers decisions, not competing inputs — the failure direction is a no-op (a placeholder survives, i.e. today's behaviour) or a lost `rtk` prefix, never a corrupted command.

✅ **`typos-check` is now a DECIDED-NO, not a pending item (2026-09-02 16:04 EDT).** A typo has more than one plausible correction, so it fails the one-right-value test that governs every promotion here, and a silent wrong substitution in prose is worse than a deny: a deny is visible, a bad rewrite produces a plausible file with no author. It stays advisory permanently. **Two OTHER gates were promoted instead** — `rg-flag-guard` corrects a multi-letter cluster (`-rn` → `-n`) while leaving a lone `-r` alone, and a new `merge-delete-branch-autofix.sh` appends `--delete-branch` BEFORE a merge rather than lamenting it after.

**Still to do — the other FIVE blind hooks** (`clock-inject` · `defer-in-place-guard` · `batch-edit-guard` · `spec-handoff-coauthor-nudge`, plus one inline `PostToolUse Edit|Write` hook). ⚠️ **This line said "four" while listing five, and the five it listed included `typos-check`, which is a decided-NO rather than pending — caught 2026-09-02 16:26 EDT by the completeness sweep, not by any gate.** `typos-check` stays advisory permanently and is NOT part of this remainder; the inline hook is, and had been dropped from the count. 🔴 **`FileChanged` CANNOT DO THIS, AND MY APPROVED PLAN SAID IT COULD — corrected 2026-09-02 11:44 EDT.** Its matcher *"is split on `|` and each segment is registered as a **literal filename in the working directory**… Regex patterns are not useful here: a value like `^\.env` would watch a file literally named `^\.env`."* So it is a per-file watch list, not a glob, and it cannot cover *whatever file a heredoc happens to write*. It stays the right tool for a **known, named** file (a config that must be reloaded when anything rewrites it) and is the wrong tool for general write coverage. **The working mechanism is the one `timestamp-check` already proved: add `Bash` to the matcher and read `.tool_input.command`.** I read that matcher paragraph, quoted the adjacent sentence about firing regardless of the writer, and did not join them — the sentence is true and the conclusion I drew from it was not. Each of the five needs its own judgement about whether firing on every Bash call is worth it, or whether it should gate on the command looking like a write — that is why they are not a batch. ✅ **Of the four promotions this entry originally named, TWO shipped and TWO did not — recorded here because the sweep found them about to disappear along with the wording that named them (2026-09-02 16:26 EDT).**

- ✅ `rg-flag-guard` — ships, and corrects a multi-letter cluster only.
- ✅ `gh pr merge --delete-branch` — ships as `merge-delete-branch-autofix.sh`.
- ❌ **`notes-hardwrap-check` — NOT DONE, and harder than it looked.** It is a `PostToolUse` check, and that event can only replace a tool's RESULT, never the file already written — so correcting there is impossible without moving it to `PreToolUse` first. And `scripts/reflow-prose.mjs` works on a WHOLE file with front-matter awareness; running it on a `new_string` fragment is a different operation and could reflow across a boundary it cannot see. **Low value regardless**: `npm run docs:reflow` already gates this in the suite, so the defect cannot ship — it only costs an extra edit. Do it alongside the fragment-vs-file question, not before.
- ❌ **The co-author trailer block — NOT DONE, and probably should not be.** `squash-trailer-gate.sh` DENIES a `--squash` merge with no `--body`, and appending trailers to a body the hook did not compose would mean inventing the body. The trailers are a fixed literal; the body they belong at the end of is not, and that is the half that fails the one-right-value test.

**Verify:** a `python3` heredoc writing `HH:xx` comes back **corrected**, not reported; `bash .claude/hooks/run-all-tests.sh` stays green with coverage for every new script.

### ✅ `[CLOSED 2026-09-09 10:19 EDT]` Access — an admin's NOTE can be edited, two ways, through one gate

🔴 **" + "BUILDING THIS FOUND A LIVE DATA-LOSS BUG ON THE SAME PATH — FIXED 2026-09-09 09:55 EDT.** `portal/ui/access.js`'s `confirmSave` called `grant()` with **three arguments where four are declared**, so `note` was `undefined`, `JSON.stringify` dropped the key, and the route's `note: body.note || ''` turned that absence into `''` — written as a `$set`, because the update document carries no operators. **Every permission edit from the grid silently erased that admin's label**, under a toast reading *"Permissions saved."* The label is the only thing telling `…000001` from `…000003` on a screen of snowflakes, so adjusting somebody's permissions destroyed the only way to tell who they were. **Fixed in three places:** the server's new `adminGrantDoc()` omits `note` unless it is actually a string (so absence never clears, while a deliberate `''` still does), the call site passes `admin.note` so the intent is visible, and `scripts/portalApi.test.js` gained four cases plus a **call-site arity scan** — proven able to fail by putting the real regression back, which it reports as `access.js:448 passes 3`.

⚠️ **NOTHING COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT, and that is its own filed gap:** `portal/ui/harness/stub.js` serves the Access **GET**s and has **no `/api/access/grant` handler at all**, so the realm's whole write path is unreachable from the harness. No rendered walk, no states registry and no overlay diff exercises it. **Do:** give the stub a grant/revoke handler that mutates its in-memory `FIX.accessAdmins`, so a save is observable. **Verify by:** driving a cell toggle and row Save in the harness and seeing the row's own label survive it — the reproduction that was not available today.

✅ **BUILT 2026-09-09 10:19 EDT — Harkirat chose the hybrid: both entry points, one commit gate.** The premise of the first pop-up was false and had to be corrected before he could answer it: the permissions half was never missing (`access.js:169` — the grid cells already stage and the row's Save already opens the typed confirm), so the only field that could not be edited was the label.

| Half | What it does |
|---|---|
| **In the row** | The label is a button; clicking it swaps to an input that stages exactly like a permission cell. The avatar initial follows what is being typed, the row's Save count includes it, and Discard drops it. A label-only save reaches the same typed confirm — retitled **Rename …000002?** with a **Save the label** action, because "Change what …000002 can do?" over a rename is one body describing the wrong act |
| **In the drawer** | Each row gets the design's **Edit** chip, opening `GrantForm` in edit mode: the id fixed and read-only, the Discord preview card resolving so you can see WHICH human the digits are, the permission chips pre-filled from what they hold, the label pre-filled, and the same typed confirmation. **Save changes** |

🔴 **THE EDIT MODE SHIPPED BROKEN ONCE, IN THE FIRST DRAFT, AND ONLY THE BROWSER SAW IT.** It faked the Discord lookup as `{ status: 'ok' }` to skip a round-trip it judged pointless — and the preview reads `lookup.user.avatarUrl`, so the drawer threw before painting anything. `npm test` was green over it. The reasoning was wrong twice: the avatar and username are exactly what tell you which human a row of digits is, which is the argument that put the lookup on the grant form in the first place. It runs in both modes now and gates readiness in neither — an id already in the grid was resolved when it was granted, and making a rename wait on Discord being reachable would let an outage there lock the owner out of fixing a typo here.

**Three states registered in `portal/fixtures/states/access.json`**, and the middle one is the instrument that would have caught the crash: the Edit chip opening a pre-filled form · **the preview card resolving for the row being edited** · the label editing in place and the row offering to save it. **Proven able to fail** by putting the faked lookup back — the walk goes red, and green again when it is removed. ⚠️ **SSR could not have caught it**: `portalRender.test.js` never runs the effect, so the lookup stays `idle` and the preview is never reached.

⚠️ **Two more defects the browser found and no gate did:** the `autofocus` attribute is honoured only while a page is being parsed, so clicking the label swapped in an input and left the caret where it was (a callback ref with an already-focused guard now, so it does not drag the caret on every keystroke); and a backtick inside an HTML comment inside an `html` template closes the literal — the trap `.claude/rules/portal-editing.md` already names, walked into anyway. *Filed 2026-09-01 18:26 EDT by the Access conformance pass (Part 4).*

The Access grid edits an admin's PERMISSIONS by clicking cells and saving the row, which is the whole of what `/api/access/grant` changes. The design also offers an **Edit** chip per row (`docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/access.html:432`) opening a drawer that edits the permissions **and the note** — the free-text label that is the only thing distinguishing `…000001` from `…000003` on screen. **The portal can write a note at grant time and never again.** So a note that was wrong, or that describes someone whose role changed, is permanent short of revoking and re-granting.

Not built during the conformance pass because a new editor with its own fields, validation and typed confirm is a feature rather than a conformance fix, and Part 4's scope explicitly excludes those. The `button.chip` "Edit" difference is CITED in `docs/reference/portal-decision-ledger.md` § Access as the `80x176` region at `(1152,496)` and will report on every Access run until this lands.

- **🔐 Adopt `dotenvx` encryption for `.env`/`.env.dev`/the prod VM's `.env`** `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]` *(filed 2026-08-30 23:17 EDT, out of a real incident: a coding session Read the full `.env.dev` file, then separately ran `head -3` on prod's plaintext `.env` and printed the LIVE prod `BOT_TOKEN` to its own transcript trying to check whether encryption was already active. It wasn't — this item is the fix.)* `dotenvx` v2.22.0 is already installed globally on Harkirat's Mac (`/opt/homebrew/bin/dotenvx`), but this repo has never adopted it: `package.json` depends on plain `dotenv` (`^17.4.1`), every `.env*` file is stored fully plaintext, and there's no `.env.keys` file (the artifact dotenvx creates once encryption is on). **What it buys:** `dotenvx encrypt` converts a `.env` file's values to ciphertext at rest (keeping a `DOTENV_PUBLIC_KEY` header) and splits the decryption key into a separate, gitignored `.env.keys` file — so an accidental broad file read (by a human, a coding agent, or a leaked backup) shows ciphertext, not real secrets. The app boots the same way afterward, just via `dotenvx run -- node index.js` (or `dotenvx run -- node --env-file=.env.dev index.js` for the dev bot) instead of a bare `node`, and `bot/registry.js`'s/`index.js`'s existing `require('dotenv').config()` calls don't need to change. **Scope:** encrypt `/Applications/Claude Code/Diors-Builds/.env` and `.env.dev` locally, encrypt the VM's `/home/harkirat/diors-builds/.env`, update `scripts/deploy.sh`'s restart command and the `diors-bot`/`dioreo-portal` systemd units' `ExecStart` lines to go through `dotenvx run --`, and confirm `.env.keys` is gitignored on both the Mac and the VM (it must never be committed — it IS the decryption key). ⚠️ **This does not fully close the exposure risk on its own** — if a future session needs a DECRYPTED value for something (e.g. building a connection string), it still has to avoid printing it; encryption protects against a broad accidental read, not against careless use of an already-decrypted value. **Considered and explicitly NOT picked, same session:** 1Password CLI (no free tier — `op` needs a paid 1Password account, $3.99/mo minimum) and Infisical (a full self-hosted platform needing Postgres+Redis or their $18/user/month cloud — real overkill for one maintainer's `.env` files; would make sense only if secrets needed centralizing across multiple repos). **Verify by:** after encrypting, `cat .env` shows ciphertext values (not `BOT_TOKEN=MTU...` in the clear); the bot still boots cleanly via `dotenvx run -- node index.js`; `git status` shows `.env.keys` as untracked/ignored on both machines.

### ✅ `[CLOSED 2026-09-09 17:38 EDT]` `portalStatus`'s stale branch is CERTIFIED — and without the falsifier that destroyed a turn's work

**How:** the decision was extracted as an exported pure function, `isStale(lastUiCt, lastFixCt)`, so it takes two commit timestamps and needs no tree, no worktree and no `git reset --hard` — which is what the entry warned about, having cost four uncommitted edits on 2026-08-30. `scripts/portalStatus.test.mjs` (new, wired into `npm test` and `portal:status:test`) proves **five** cases: stale fires when `portal/ui` is newer; fresh when the fixture is newer; **fresh when the two are equal**, which is the real tree's own shape since a fixture and the change it measures are committed together; a missing timestamp is never stale, so a fresh clone cannot cry wolf on all seven; and the comparison is numeric rather than lexical.

⚠️ **The end-to-end condition this entry originally named is NOT what was run.** It asked for a tree where `portal/ui` has a commit newer than a fixture. The unit above certifies the decision rather than the plumbing around it — `git log -1 --format=%ct` reading the right paths is still only proven by the fresh branch. That is a smaller claim than the original wording and is stated rather than glossed.

*Original entry:* *Filed 2026-08-30 16:0x EDT. Named in `96faa38`'s message and in no list until now.*

`scripts/portalStatus.mjs` reports per realm whether `portal/ui` has moved since its geometry fixture was recorded. **The FRESH branch is proven** (all seven report fresh on a tree where nothing moved). **The STALE branch has never fired**, so by this repo's own rule — prove a probe can report PRESENCE before trusting its silence — it is uncertified. Its first version cried stale on all seven at once (a fixture cannot stamp the commit it is about to be committed in); that false positive is fixed and the true positive is untested.

⚠️ **The safe falsifier matters here.** The obvious one — commit a real `portal/ui` change, run it, `git reset --hard` back — is what **destroyed four uncommitted edits on 2026-08-30 15:3x EDT**, including a plan correction that left a wrong instruction live for minutes. **Do not test it that way.** Test it in a scratch clone or a throwaway worktree, or by pointing the two `git log -1 --format=%ct` reads at fixture paths with known-different commit times.

The 2026-08-28 pivot — match the mockup first, rebuild redesigns on top — was a good call for the problem it solved: Part 1 had been closed and reopened three times on adjudication-by-judgement, and a number ended that. **Its premise was that the mockup is a fixed reference that can be converged on without arguing.** Three things hit that premise on 2026-08-30: the mockup has visual defects that were faithfully reproduced (unstyled day list with browser bullets, 3px focusable ruler ticks, a doubled ring); `DESIGN.md` turned out to be generated from the *superseded* package; and two "portal defects" from another session were artefacts of that same retired source. **Four wrong conclusions in one day, all from a reference that is less fixed than the pivot assumed.**

**What is NOT in dispute:** the method works, the instruments are good, and Season genuinely matches. **What is:** whether "matches the mockup" is the right definition of done for the remaining five realms, or whether the pass should now (a) fix the mockup where it is wrong before converging on it, (b) converge to a coarser threshold and spend the difference on reachability/real-server correctness, or (c) continue as-is. **Harkirat's call, not mine.** Cost input: ~200 turns bought one realm and change; armory is 13.6% / 729 regions.

What is still uncovered, verbatim from that list: **viewport 1282×888 only (375×812 never run on any realm)**; **data states — empty, error, loading — are walked by `portal:states` but never PIXEL-compared**, which is two instruments that have never been pointed at each other; **transitions are zeroed**, so only the settled frame is compared; **light mode is out of scope by decision.**


### 🧭 `portal:status` prints a realm's DATA ROWS as its views, run together into one word `P3 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium`
*Found 2026-08-30 18:19 EDT by reading the tool's own output while establishing this session's floor.*

The `views` column for **review** reads `T1Season identityseason.setTitlesDeadlines · season · T1The Widow's Bite Drawdraw.edit · seasonneeds attention · …` — two defects in one cell. Review's mockup has **zero view tabs** (its own row in the plan says so), so the scraper is picking up changeset rows and calling them views; and the labels it scrapes are fused exactly like the item above, which is the same htm-whitespace class seen from the outside.

⚠️ **The row is also the widest in the table and pushes the `portal/ui commits since` column off the end**, so that column's sentence renders truncated as *"✅ fresh — portal/ui unchanged since"* with no commit after it — a status line that cannot state its own subject. ⚠️ **And `fresh` is computed from COMMITS only**: it read `✅ fresh — portal/ui unchanged since 5311b54` while `portal/ui/season.js` was modified in the working tree. A freshness claim that cannot see an uncommitted edit is the kind that goes quietly wrong.

**Fix:** scrape view labels from the mockup's view-tab control specifically rather than from whatever matches, join scraped text with a space, truncate the column, and let the freshness check consider `git status` as well as the commit range.
**Verify:** review's `views` cell reads `(single view)` or names its real tabs, no cell truncates the column beside it, and a dirty `portal/ui` reports as dirty rather than fresh.

### ✅ ⏰ `portalGeometry` does not freeze its clock — **FIXED 2026-08-31 09:4x EDT** `P2 · S · Sonnet5-High`

**It became reproducible the day the mode collapse landed, and it was nearly buried by a re-record.** The season clock used to read from the start of a fixture day — refused as class (b), so it now reads `Date.now()` — and a live countdown changes the WIDTH of its readout as digits roll, so `sizeIssues` flickered by exactly one and a `--write` disagreed with a `--check` run seconds later. 🔴 **The tell was that two consecutive checks agreed with each other and both disagreed with the fixture: stable, so not noise; disagreeing, so not the page.** `scripts/portalGeometry.mjs` now freezes `Date` at the same instant `portalDiff` pins (`2026-08-24T18:41:00Z`, the mockup's own `F.today`) and clears storage before any page script runs, since one page is reused across every realm and view. **Proven by three consecutive `--all --check` runs with zero drift**, not by one green run. ⚠️ **A re-record would have made this disappear while leaving the cause in place** — §0.45's *"re-recording a baseline is not a gate passing"*, met in the wild.

**Original entry, for the record:** *Found 2026-08-30 22:0x EDT by the one full verification run, not by a gate designed to catch it.*

`scripts/portalDiff.mjs` pins `Date` on both sides before any page script runs — the whole reason its residual has no unexplained floor. **`scripts/portalGeometry.mjs` does not.** It navigates with a cache-buster and reads whatever the wall clock says.

So any recorded value derived from *today* drifts without a code change. Measured: Home's fixture failed with `sections: … Stops by then 7 … → … Stops by then 6 …`, and `home.js:131` renders `Stops by then <b>${ending.length}</b>` — a count of what ends before the next deadline. One item ended since the fixture was written. Nothing in the portal changed.

🔴 **This is the same class as everything else this session's instruments were corrected for: a difference reported that is not a difference.** It is worse here than a false positive, because the remedy on offer is `--write`, and re-recording makes it go away without anyone learning why — the tool's own message even suggests it. §0.45 already warns that re-recording a baseline is not a gate passing; this is the case that proves it.

**What to do:** mirror `portalDiff`'s `evaluateOnNewDocument` Date shim into `portalGeometry`, with the same `--at 2026-08-24` default so the two instruments measure under one clock. ⚠️ **It invalidates every existing fixture** — all seven were recorded unfrozen — so the change and a full `--all --write` belong in the same commit, with the before/after diffed to confirm only date-derived values moved.
**Verify:** record a fixture, wait past a boundary the data crosses (or fake it with `--at`), re-check, and confirm it still matches. Today that check fails.

### Events and Playlists auto-collapse on real data, hiding 20 of the season's 39 items `[P2 · S · Opus5-Medium]`

🔴 **MEASURED 2026-09-06 17:46 EDT AND THE CLAIM DOES NOT REPRODUCE. Adjudicated: THE RULE IS CORRECT, NO CHANGE.**

**The entry counted ITEMS; the rule counts ROWS.** `track.js:673` is `assignRows(...).rows > 3`, and `assignRows` packs non-overlapping spans onto one row — so a lane with many items that do not overlap occupies few rows and never collapses. Read from `data-lane`/`data-rows`/`.lnc` in the harness at 1282px:

| Lane | rows | collapsed | items |
|---|---|---|---|
| draw | 1 | no | 3 |
| returning | 1 | no | **11** |
| drawwindow | 3 | no | 3 |
| **event** | **2** | **no** | **6** |
| **playlist** | **7** | **yes** | — |

**Events does NOT collapse** — six items occupy two rows. **Four of five lanes are open and 23 items are visible.** Only Playlists collapses, at seven rows, which is 192px against 30px and is exactly the wall the rule exists for. The headline "hiding 20 of the season's 39 items" is false as stated.

⚠️ **And a collapsed lane is not silent:** `track.js:269` renders `LaneSummary`, under a comment that already anticipated this — *"a collapsed lane must still ANSWER something; a row that only says '5 hidden' is a worse version of nothing."*

**The lesson, which is worth more than the item:** the claim was written from item counts because item counts are what the fixture data shows, and the rule's own threshold was never read. **One `data-rows` measurement refuted a P2 that had been open since 2026-08-28.** Original entry follows.

~~`collapsedFor` in `portal/ui/track.js` opens a lane collapsed when it needs more than three rows, *"you should not land on a wall with the lane you came for off-screen."* Against real volumes that is Events (6) and Playlists (14) — the majority of the season, closed by default, where `season.html` shows both open and populated.~~

### ⚠️ `http://127.0.0.1:8787/auth/callback` is probably not registered on the Discord app `[P3 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium]`

*Filed 2026-08-28, and this one is MINE — introduced while fixing a different trap.* The OAuth redirect now derives from the request origin against an allowlist, and that allowlist offers **both** `localhost` and `127.0.0.1`. Only `http://localhost:8787/auth/callback` is registered on the `Dioreo (Dev)` application. Opening the portal on `127.0.0.1` will therefore fail **at Discord** with `invalid redirect_uri` — so the error arrives from outside this repo and the next person debugging it starts in the wrong place. Either register the second URI or drop it from `allowedOrigins()`.

### The "a staged thing arrives" motion is styled and emitted nowhere `[P3 · S · Sonnet5-Medium]`

*Filed 2026-08-28, Part 1's reverse-orphan triage. Belongs to **motion-as-a-system (Part 7)**, which COMPANION warns is NOT the liveliness work item K closed — conflating them is how this entry gets wrongly ticked.*

Three rule sets, one idea, no emitter anywhere in `portal/ui`: **`.bar.arriving` / `.pt.arriving`** (the Track's staged bar and point) and **`tr.rowin`** (the manifest's staged row, whose own comment says *"the same way a staged bar does on the Track"*). **`.rise`** is the fourth — the orchestrated page load-in, applied in the mockup to the masthead with a 55ms-per-item stagger; `.lane`'s `laneIn` half of that sequence does run, so the page arrives in pieces.

⚠️ **Do not fix these one at a time.** They are one behaviour — *a thing you just staged announces itself* — and building the Track's half without the manifest's is what made them disagree in the first place.

### ~~🎛️ Four of §16's eight liveliness micro-interactions never reached `portal/ui`~~ ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-27 12:4x EDT** `[P2 · S]`
*Filed 2026-08-27 00:04 EDT. Found by reading the COMPANION against the code, not by any gate — `portal:coverage` reads 100% with all four absent.*

✅ **CLOSED — and it was two different findings wearing one label, which is why the count said four.**

🔴 **`.count-bump` and `.staged-pulse` were genuinely absent. Now built.** `pulseTray()` lives in `portal/ui/shell.js` (the module that owns both targets — the rail's staged count and the tray) and is called from **one place**: `composeClient.js`'s `stageOps`, the single funnel every staging path in the portal goes through, which is the same reason the mockup puts its own call inside `Store.add`. ⚠️ **Only on a real stage** — a refusal, an expired session and a validation failure all return through that same call, and pulsing for work that was not staged is a surface telling the reader something happened when nothing did. **Verified in the browser both ways:** a real stage bumps the badge with the real `countBump` animation; an injected `?fail=/api/changeset` bumps nothing.

⚠️ **The badge could not simply be bumped when the stage returns, and the first version got this wrong twice.** The mockup's store is synchronous so its count is already updated; here a stage is a round trip plus a refetch plus a re-render, and at 0→1 the badge node does not exist yet. Waiting for the count to *change* was the second wrong answer: staging into an EXISTING changeset adds an op and leaves the count alone, a real and common case that trigger would never fire for. It now bumps as soon as the badge is on the page.

🟢 **`.lift` and `.tint` were never actually missing — the tracker was reading class NAMES, not behaviour.** `portal/ui/app.css`'s own *LIVELINESS, APPLIED* block states the decision in capitals: applied **by selector, not by adding a class at every call site**, because 40 hand-added classes is 40 a new surface forgets. Measured on the live page: Armory's rack cards (`.bgrp`) lift and tint on hover through their own component rule, and the enumerated card list plus the `[style*="--c"]` hover rule cover the rest. A source scanner looking for the literal strings `lift`/`tint` in markup reports absence for a behaviour that is present — the same blind spot as every other portal gate, in the opposite direction.

⚠️ **One genuine defect the measurement did surface, and it is the `.stat.live` shape again:** `.wcard` appears in that enumerated card list in BOTH codebases and is emitted by **nothing, in either** — a name transcribed from the mockup's stylesheet for a component the portal calls something else. Filed rather than silently deleted because the enumeration is the design's own list and pruning it is a design edit.

The mockup's §16 liveliness layer was built 2026-08-25 and the CSS came across whole with `app.css`. **The markup that triggers half of it did not.** Measured: the rules exist in `portal/ui/app.css`; nothing in `portal/ui/*.js` emits them.

| Class | What it is | Why coverage cannot see it |
|---|---|---|
| `.lift` | Hover/focus raise on a card — `transform` + background, `.lift` in `app.css` | It is a class you put on markup, and it is not in any mockup PAGE's own class set, so it never entered `want` |
| `.tint` | Hover/focus tint in the element's own topic colour — `.tint` in `app.css` | Same |
| `.count-bump` | The rail badge counting up when something stages — `.count-bump` in `app.css` | Applied by `Shell.pulseTray()` via `classList.add`, which the scanner deliberately does not read |
| `.staged-pulse` | The tray acknowledging a stage — `.staged-pulse` in `app.css` | Same |

✅ **What DID cross over:** `.landed`, `Shell.arrive()`, `.mh-stats .v.rolling` and `.fdelta` are all live in `portal/ui`.

🔴 **`pulseTray` is the interesting half.** Its whole point is that every staging path funnels through one place, so the acknowledgement is written once rather than remembered at each call site. The portal stages from six realms and has no equivalent — the only acknowledgement is a toast, which is the exact defect §16.1 item 2 was written to fix, one level up.

**Verify condition:** stage something in each realm and see the rail badge bump and the tray pulse; hover a card and see it lift. ⚠️ **Not "the classes appear in source"** — that is what already reads as done.


### ~~Wire the portal harness into `npm test` — a gate that OBSERVES instead of scanning~~ ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-27 12:2x EDT** `[P1 · M]`
*Filed 2026-08-26 22:24 EDT, after an audit found a feature that could never render while every gate stayed green.*

✅ **CLOSED — `scripts/portalHarnessRender.test.js`, wired into `npm test`.** It mounts all seven realms into a real (linkedom) document with preact's own `render()`, lets each `useEffect` fire, lets the harness stub resolve, and asserts the realm got PAST its skeleton. **Proved it can fail before trusting it:** the Armory `data`-undefined bug was reinstated, the gate failed with exit 1 and named *armory* alone, and it was reverted — a gate that has never failed is not known to work.

🔴 **The reason it could not be an SSR test, which is the non-obvious part.** Every realm loads through `useAsync`, and `useAsync` fetches inside a `useEffect`. `preact-render-to-string` does not run effects, so an SSR render of any realm reaches the LOADING skeleton and stops — the loaded branch, where all three known instances of this bug class lived, is never evaluated. `scripts/portalRender.test.js` is SSR and is right to be; this one needs a DOM, so `linkedom` is now a devDependency.

⚠️ **A non-empty tree is NOT the assertion, and that was nearly the whole defect.** A realm whose loader failed still renders the shell and a skeleton, which is a perfectly valid non-empty tree — and is exactly what the dead Armory looked like for a day. The check requires the masthead, and it collects preact's `_catchError` path, `uncaughtException`, `unhandledRejection` and `console.error` output, because a component that throws during render does not reliably reject a promise.

⚠️ **It is NOT `scripts/portalHarness.test.js`**, which already existed and was already in `npm test`: that one executes the harness STUB and checks payload SHAPE, and renders no component. A reader who greps for "portalHarness" and stops at the first hit concludes this work was already done.

**The gap:** every portal gate is a SOURCE SCANNER. `portal:orphans` asks whether a class has a rule; `portal:coverage` asks whether a class name appears in a file. Neither can know whether the branch containing it ever executes. Measured on this branch: the Track's double-CP window read `data.calendar`, a key that does not exist on that prop, and rendered nothing forever — with `orphans` 0, `refs` 0, coverage counting the class as covered, and `npm test` green.

**Why more scanning will not fix it:** four scanner blind spots were closed in one session and this defect is invisible to all four by construction. The repo has exactly one gate that observes rendered output (`portalContrastRendered.test.js`, puppeteer) and it was added only after "no browser dependency" was disproved as a reason.

**What exists already:** `portal/ui/harness/` builds `portal/public/harness.html`, stubs `fetchJson` through an import map, and carries `?today=` · `?realms=` · `?owner=0` · `?empty=1` · `?fail=` · `?slow=` · `?destroy=1` · `?draft=1`. It is a complete fixture rig that nothing automated ever opens.

**Verify condition:** `npm test` fails when a component's markup is present in source but absent from the rendered tree for a fixture that must produce it. The single check added on 2026-08-26 (`a double-CP calendar item draws its window on the Track`, in `portalRender.test.js`) is the shape — it was proved by reinstating the bug and watching it fail. This item is to make that shape systematic rather than one-off.

⚠️ **Do not close this by adding another source scan.**

🔴 **A second, worse instance confirmed the premise 2026-08-27 10:15 EDT** — first time the harness was opened in a browser at all, Armory threw `ReferenceError: data is not defined` on every single load and never rendered past the skeleton (`portal/ui/armory.js`, a bare `data` copy-pasted from `broadcast.js`'s `const data = load.data` binding, which `armory.js` never has). This was not a dead branch or an invisible sub-feature like the double-CP window above — it was the **entire realm**, 133 builds, permanently unreachable, since the commit that added the staged-count masthead figure (`7f8ac19`, 2026-08-26), through two subsequent self-audits that were specifically hunting this bug class. `portalCoverage`/`orphans`/`refs` all read it as fine the whole time. Fixed same session (`8a21f32`). **Verify condition unchanged, but now doubly evidenced — do not let a third one happen before this is built.**









- `[P2 · M · Opus5-High]` **`/api/analytics` returns 495 KB on every Analytics page load.** Measured 2026-08-26 11:2x EDT against the real dev database — the first time any route had been called for real. Without it, "staged against a record that has since moved" is undetectable, and a review screen that reported unchecked as clean would be asserting something it never looked at. `stalenessOf` returns `checked:false` for changesets that predate the field. 13 cases in `scripts/portalReview.test.js`, wired into `npm test`. *Closed the same day it was filed.*

- `[P1 · XS]` ~~**TWO SOURCES DISAGREED ABOUT THE TRACK'S TOPIC COLOURS.**~~ ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-26 08:39 EDT — Harkirat: "Keep the mockup colors."** `--draw/--ret/--ev/--play` now carry `#AE72E0`/`#E8639B`/`#4A90D9`/`#2CC4C4`, and the fixture `LANES` hexes were verified identical rather than left as a second source, so the loser does not exist any more. Verified live: the Track renders purple draws, pink returning, blue events, teal playlists, and `portalContrastAudit` clears 222 pairs on the new values. The original finding is preserved below because it records HOW two internally-consistent sources hid from every gate. The design tokens say `--draw:#FF3430` (red), `--ret:#337BA6` (blue), `--ev:#1F8A5E` (green), `--play:#8A6BD1` (purple). The mockup's own `fixtures.js` `LANES` array carries a **different** set — `#AE72E0` purple, `#E8639B` pink, `#4A90D9` blue, `#2CC4C4` teal — and the mockup's Track renders from *those*, via `lane.bar`, not from the tokens. So the approved-looking screenshots were painted by a fixture file. `portal/ui/track.js` uses the **tokens**, because production cannot read lane identity out of a fixture, which means the live Track is now red/blue/green/purple where the mockup was purple/pink/blue/teal. **Colour carries topic (§4.1), so this is identity, not decoration — it needs your eye, not a coin flip.** Whichever wins, the loser should stop existing: if the fixture hexes are right they become the token values; if the tokens are right the `LANES` hexes are deleted so nothing renders from them again. *Found 2026-08-25 23:5x EDT while migrating the Track onto the adopted design; the divergence is invisible until the two are rendered side by side, because each is internally consistent.*

- `[P2 · M · Opus5-High]` **A `/portal` slash command that signs you in and hands you the browser.** *Harkirat's idea, 2026-08-25 14:0x EDT — "just an idea for now, worth discussing/designing in a future session some time." Recorded with its design questions rather than as a one-liner, because the interesting part is not the command, it is what the command lets you skip.*

  **The shape he described:** a `/portal` command, visible only to people whose permissions allow portal use, that runs the portal's auth inside the Discord session you are already in and then sends you to the portal in a browser, already signed in.

  🔴 **"Visible only" is not literally achievable and that has to be designed around, not discovered later.** A bot **cannot hide a global slash command from one user or one guild** — the same constraint `.claude/rules/admin-controls.md` documents for `/admin`, and the reason nothing in this project "refuses" a command by hiding it. Two real options: register it `[1]` (user-install only), like `/manage`, `/bot` and `/autobuild`, so it never appears in a guild it was not installed into; or register it public and have it answer a non-admin with a plain ephemeral refusal that says what it is and who it is for. **The first matches how every other admin command here already behaves.**

  ✨ **The part worth building it for:** the bot already knows who invoked the command. `portal/auth.js` currently runs a full Discord OAuth round trip to establish that same fact — so `/portal` could **skip OAuth entirely** and mint a short-lived, single-use sign-in link against the invoking user's id, landing them in the portal already authenticated. That is a genuine "the portal can do that?" moment rather than a shortcut to an existing button.

  ⚠️ **And that is exactly why it needs a design pass rather than an afternoon.** A sign-in link in a Discord message **is a bearer credential**: anyone holding the string is the user. Non-negotiables for any version of this — the reply is **ephemeral**, the token is **single-use**, the TTL is **short** (a minute, not the session's 12 hours), it is bound to the invoking `discordId` so a forwarded link is useless, and consuming it creates a normal `PortalSession` rather than becoming one. ⚠️ It also puts a **new stored per-user artifact** in the system, so `docs/legal/PRIVACY.md` §2 and Appendix A move in the same change — this repo's own `privacy-model-coverage` check exists for precisely that.

  **Open questions for the design session:** does it deep-link to a realm (`/portal season`)? Does it work in a guild the bot is in, or only in DMs? Does it replace the Door's Discord button or sit alongside it? And does a portal session opened this way differ from a normal one in what it may do — the `destructive` permission is owner-grantable and one-way ops need an export, so "signed in from Discord" must not become a second, weaker authorisation path.

  **Verify by:** a design doc in `docs/superpowers/specs/` before any code, per the plan-drafting rule — this one has a real premise risk (the visibility constraint) and a silent failure mode (a leaked link), which is exactly the shape the falsification pass exists for.

- `[P3 · M · Opus5-High]` **Make MOTION a system across all eight portal realms, instead of Season-only.** *Filed 2026-08-25 12:5x EDT on Harkirat's explicit instruction — "file the motion option as something to consider for the future but not necessary at the moment."*

  Motion in the portal mockups is **Season-only by a recorded decision** (COMPANION §5.9l.4d: a permission matrix has no geometry a reader tracks, so animating it would be decoration). That reasoning was right for a target of *correct*. It is the wrong answer for the target Harkirat set on 2026-08-25 — *"does it push every aspect of the website to it's upper echelon?"* — because seven realms currently have **no arrival, no state transition, and nothing that says the interface is alive**. `tokens.css` already declares the whole system (`--ease` plus `--dur-1/2/3`); it simply goes unused outside Season.

  ⚠️ **This is NOT the same thing as the subtle-liveliness work he asked for in the same breath, and conflating the two is how this entry gets wrongly closed.** He wants *"subtle animations, liveliness to elements, and hints/touches of magic scattered to elements, pages, features"* — opportunistic, per-element delight, being built now. **This entry is the structural question**: does motion become a declared system with rules about what may move and why, applied uniformly, overturning §5.9l.4d? That is a design-language decision, and it is his.

  **What to do when it is picked up:** state the rule first (what motion is FOR — this package's own answer is *"motion earns its place by explaining where something came from"*), then audit each realm against it, then build. ⚠️ **Audit rule 14 must hold throughout** — every new `animation` needs a `prefers-reduced-motion` override or the gate fails, which is exactly what it is for.

  **Verify by:** a written per-realm decision in `COMPANION.md` (in the shape of §5.9p), `.states.html?self=1` still ALL CLEAN, and PASS 4f still reporting zero running animations under lifted reduced-motion rules on all eight pages.

- **The Armory fixtures are hand-typed under a comment claiming they are exported** `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-High]` *(filed 2026-08-24 15:4x EDT, from the code review of the real-schema pass.)* `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/assets/fixtures.js`'s `LOADOUT_STATS` and its 36 `builds` sit BELOW the exported block under a header reading *"⚠️ THESE ARE REAL DOCUMENTS, exported from the dev collection … not invented"* — and `.export-fixtures.mjs` emits **nothing** for them. They were exported by hand once, in a previous session, and cannot be regenerated by the script the file tells you to run. So the realm with the worst history of invented fields is the one realm with neither export coverage nor gate coverage, and §3.9.1's *"everything below that block is derived from it, so no fact is stored twice"* is false for the largest block in the file. **What to do:** extend `.export-fixtures.mjs` to emit `builds`, `LOADOUT_STATS` and the analytics aggregates the same way it emits the Season/Broadcast/Access block, then add them to `.schema-gate.mjs`'s `checkFixtureShape` cases against `models/Loadout.js`. **Verify:** delete the hand-typed block, re-run the export, and confirm `npm run portal:gate` and all 8 page audits still pass — if the regenerated block differs from what was there, the difference is the drift this entry is about.

- **`.schema-gate.mjs` checks field NAMES and never field VALUES** `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-High]` *(filed 2026-08-24 15:4x EDT, same review.)* Its own header names four errors it exists to prevent — and **one of them, the fictional `editor` role, would pass all four of its checks**, because a role is a VALUE, not a field. So would `categoryRank:'S'`, `mode:'BR'`, `category:'sale'` or a retired permission token like `'alerts'`. Three further gaps, in order of how likely each is to bite: `checkFixtureShape` samples only `[0]` of each array, so an invented field on row 5 is invisible; four fixture blocks have no model behind them at all (`builds[]`, `accessAdmins[]`, `changeLogRows[]`, `draft`); and `COMPANION.md` is not scanned, which is why a stale `access.setScope` survived in it until a human reviewer read the file. **What to do:** add a value check driven by the real enums — `SeasonalData.calendar.category`, `Loadout.mode`, the tier vocabulary from `utils/adminParser.js`, `MANAGE_PAGE_SCOPES` — and widen the shape check to every row rather than the first. **Verify:** the `--self-test` probe list must gain a value-probe that FAILS before the check exists (e.g. `category:'sale'`), the same way the four existing probes do; a gate whose new check cannot be made to fail is worse than not adding it.

- **`announcementState` is computed in the export and re-derived in the page** `[P3 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium]` *(filed 2026-08-24 15:4x EDT, same review.)* `.export-fixtures.mjs` stamps `state` onto every announcement using the real `portal/api/broadcast.js` function, and `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/broadcast.html`'s `recompute()` derives it again inline. The two agree today. `portal/api/broadcast.js`'s own header states the rule being broken: *"Computed server-side … so the pill, the ordering and the Now-showing membership all read the same answer — three client-side re-derivations is three chances to drift."* The re-derivation is genuinely needed (the edit drawer changes `expiresAt`/`startsAt` live), so the fix is to move it into `fixtures.js` beside `isEventEnded` and have the page call it, not to delete it. **Verify:** `rg "state = " broadcast.html` returns nothing, and editing an announcement's expiry in the drawer still moves it between the live/scheduled/expired filters.

- **Verify `prefers-reduced-motion` actually takes effect in the portal mockups** `[P3 · XS · Sonnet5-Medium]` *(filed 2026-08-24 11:05 EDT, out of the falsification pass on the interactive portal package.)* `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/assets/app.css` carries **six** `@media (prefers-reduced-motion:reduce)` blocks covering the view-switch, staging, tier-drop and entrance animations. The syntax is right and nothing has ever observed them working — no tool available in that session could emulate the preference, so this is **unverified, not done**, and the COMPANION says so explicitly rather than letting the CSS's existence stand in for evidence. **What to do:** open each of the eight pages with the preference forced on — Chrome DevTools *Rendering → Emulate CSS prefers-reduced-motion*, or `--force-prefers-reduced-motion` — and confirm every animated affordance still *communicates* without moving. **How to verify:** with the emulation on, call `getAnimations()` on `.bcol`, `.trow`, `.nscard`, `.tile` and `.lane` and assert it returns empty, **and** confirm each element is still visible at rest — the failure mode is not "it still animates", it is "the reveal was the animation, so disabling it leaves the content invisible", which is the `opacity:0`-as-resting-state trap COMPANION §14 already documents.
- **Analytics cannot answer "everything that touched draws this week"** `[P3 · M · Opus5-Medium]` *(filed 2026-08-24 11:05 EDT; split out of a two-part entry on 2026-08-24 13:1x EDT when its other half turned out to be fiction rather than a gap — see the archive.)* The event river filters by kind and by actor, never by **subject**, so the realm's own promise — *what happened* — is only two-thirds delivered. Needs a subject dimension on the event fixture and a third chip set. **How to verify:** filter the river to a subject and confirm the count matches what the owning realm reports for the same window. ⚠️ *This clause used to open with "grant an editor a scope" — an orphan left by the split, and a re-introduction of the exact fiction the archive entry warns against. There is no editor role; there never was.*
- **Season's data has three real defects the portal can now see — decide what to do about each** `[P2 · S · Sonnet5-High]` *(filed 2026-08-24 13:0x EDT, out of the real-schema rebuild. These are DATA findings, not code: the new Season Repairs view reports them against the live `SeasonalData` document and they are still there.)* **(a)** *Two of the three `/calendar` banners are signed `media.discordapp.net` URLs* — they carry an `ex=` expiry parameter and 404 once it passes. `utils/calendarBannerCache.js` exists to re-host these through Cloudinary and only the playlists banner ever went through it. **(b)** *"Attack of the Undead MP Mode" is in `calendar[]` twice* with identical dates, so `/calendar` renders the entry twice. **(c)** *11 of the 14 draws have no explicit calendar row*, so `commands/calendar.js` serves them synthetic and `dateOnly`, and `isEventEnded()` returns false for them **forever** — they leave `/calendar` only when a season rollover drops them from the array. (c) is by design and may well be what you want; (a) and (b) are not. **How to verify:** open Season → Repairs in the mockup against a live DB, or re-derive with the predicates in `docs/superpowers/mockups/2026-08-23-portal-interactive/assets/fixtures.js`'s `seasonRepairs()`.
